The verb is "передал" (passed/handed over).
The structure is: Subject (Пётр) + Verb (передал) + Direct Object (книгу) + Indirect Object (Марии).
In Russian, the verb "передавать/передать" (to pass/hand over) typically takes a dative case for the recipient (to whom it is passed).
"Марии" is the dative form of "Мария".
Therefore, "Марии" functions as the recipient of the action "передал".

The word in question is the second "Марии" (inside the quote).
The verb is "позвонил" (called).
In Russian, the verb "звонить/позвонить" (to call) typically takes a dative case object to indicate the person being called (the recipient).
The form "Марии" can be Dative singular (to Maria) or Genitive singular (of Maria).
Given the context "Иван позвонил Марии" (Ivan called Maria), the most natural interpretation is that Ivan called Maria. Therefore, "Марии" is the recipient of the action.
Option 1 describes this as a Direct role RECIPIENT of the predicate «позвонил» with dative case.
Option 2 suggests a Genitive dependency on "Ivan", which would mean "Ivan of Maria" or similar, which doesn't fit the syntax of "Ivan called...".
Option 3 and 4 suggest dependencies on "Ivan" with dative/prepositional cases, which is grammatically incorrect for the relationship between the subject "Ivan" and the object "Maria" in this transitive construction. The object depends on the verb, not the subject.

Therefore, Option 1 is the correct grammatical attachment.
